## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-2680-opening/STATE_LEDGER.md

# Kindling 2680 opening state ledger

Status: opening locks resolved 2026-10-08. Ready for the prologue and first
eight-chapter continuous prose run.

## Prologue and opening day

- Year 2680, during the six-hundredth-anniversary season of the Fall.
- The prologue's final historical snapshot is 2676: **Kiva Rowan**, thirteen,
  reaches the Kindling uninjured and opens all three known doors.
- Chapter One begins four years later. Kiva is seventeen and seeking collegiate
  placement for the 2680 season.
- Kiva lives with her aunt and legal guardian, **Mara Rowan**, in Meridian. Mara
  repairs municipal instruments and reconciles their paper records.
- The Satori disk reached Kiva through a sealed Home Line appointment by its prior
  bearer, not through blood descent. Kiva knows it is old and entrusted; she does
  not know its full public history or origin.
- Meridian's schools expect one first door and have procedures for unusual strength,
  but no accepted process for three simultaneous traditions.

## Knowledge boundaries

- General public: knows the Fall, Kindling, ranks, schools, Riftspawn, and ordinary
  filter language as history/science appropriate to the final world-state choice.
- Students: know institutional versions of Home Line history, not necessarily the
  surviving founder records.
- Orla Dane: knows the disk family and at least one suppressed prior anomaly.
- Kiva: does not know the disk's true origin, the full filter theory, or why
  all three doors opened.
- Kiva remembers the 2676 fever dream in sensory fragments: Glory-level figures
  converging, the sky/filter absent, pure presence pressing into the world, and every
  ash-lit thing responding. She does not know the later name **Unroofed Sky**, whether
  the dream predicts anything, or that older Glory accounts overlap it.
- Orla knows there are disputed historical fever accounts but does not begin Book One
  with proof that Kiva's dream matches them.
- Reader: sees evidence before explanation; no prologue solves the mystery.

## Physical and progression state

- All three doors open at Kindling, but none is mastered.
- The event leaves three fine, asymmetrical heat-lines from Kiva's sternum toward
  her left shoulder. They remain visible and become tender when the doors compete.
- The disk warms once, only after Kiva completes the doors in a correct deliberate
  sequence. It never instructs her or grants technique.
- The disk can amplify or carry the Unroofed Sky fever dream, but it cannot trigger
  on demand, answer questions, or supply a combat/engineering solution.
- Initial classification is incomplete or contradictory, not a secret top rank.

## Social state

- Kiva has a real home/family obligation in Mara's repair-and-records work.
- Anwen Pryce, Perrin Cade, and Kellan Renn have independent placements and
  goals before meeting the protagonist.
- Institutions have defensible reasons to fear a category they cannot safely test.

## Institutional and world locks

- Campus compact: **the Meridian Compact**.
- Mixed-path college: **the Crooked Line School**.
- Resilience/body college: **Stonehand Hall**.
- Medical, Ward, Sustain, and ash-engineering college: **Wren House**.
- Collegiate competition: **the Meridian Collegiate Circuit**; final regional
  event: **the Regional Measure**. Events include individual Measures, team lanes,
  ashcraft trials, rescue/extraction problems, and controlled Riftspawn engagements.
- The collegiate curriculum includes a staged field license: contained spawn anatomy
  and kill drills, supervised Handful kills, live-rift recovery, then higher-yield team
  responses. Book One devotes several connected chapters to Kiva's team learning how
  to kill Riftspawn and recover the ash cleanly before the first major live response.
- Above college are **chartered rift companies**: salaried professional teams that
  fight Riftspawn, close or contain rifts, rescue civilians, protect infrastructure,
  and recover ash. Standings reward closure, lives saved, protected infrastructure,
  clean ash, and rule compliance—not kills alone.
- Book One is regional. Book Two expands toward national competition and company
  politics. Book Three becomes international as the filter and ash grid destabilize.
- Mature hardened regional technology and scheduled wormhole stations exist. Travel
  is expensive and bounded; interplanetary normality does not.
- Classification uses physical instruments, projected tables, and human rulings.
  There is no private retinal or software-like status overlay.

## Ash-electric civilization and Glory

- Refined ash is the dominant finite energy source. **Ash induction** converts a
  controlled ash reaction into ordinary current for lights, pumps, heat,
  communications, medicine, workshops, transit, arenas, and rift sensing.
- Ash does not create free energy. Cells are graded, metered, consumed, reconciled,
  and vulnerable to contamination or runaway.
- **Ashcraft** blends magic and ash: a trained soul supplies pattern, intention,
  and control; ownerless ash supplies most of the fuel. Different traditions shape,
  contain, conduct, or sustain the reaction differently.
- Modern cells continue running after a Kindled operator starts and tunes them, but
  still require maintenance, replacement ash, and emergency shutdown.
- At **Glory**, a rare master can impress stable patterns at civic or industrial
  scale. Glory does not make ash infinite; it expands safe throughput, duration,
  precision, and the complexity of blended systems.
- That capability produces ash-power industrial dynasties as well as school orders
  whose masters pursue near-monastic devotion to one tradition. Some orders reject
  corporate ownership; some industry houses sponsor them; some Glory masters move
  between both worlds.
- Pro rift companies feed the ash grid. This creates prestige and public service,
  but also incentives to overhunt, hide risk, manipulate ash grades, or protect
  wealthy districts first.
- A sanctioned kill produces a recorded yield. Civic power, casualty reserve, school,
  and team shares are removed in that order. Student shares fund training cells,
  metered combat techniques, equipment, travel, and medical reserve; they are not
  unmetered personal treasure.
- Ash is recorded by **yield** (Handful, Flask, Barrel, Well), **grade** (purity and
  stability), and **resonance** (which patterns can use that spawn expression safely).
  A large dirty yield may be worth less than a small stable one.
- Killing does not grant rank automatically. Real combat can deepen capacity because
  the practitioner works at the edge of owned control and must later reproduce the
  lesson. Ash funds the next threshold; training makes the threshold survivable.
- Filter thinning increases both human potential and Riftspawn pressure. The opening
  book presents this first as unusually capable students and an unusually varied rift
  season. Later books prove the common Glory-pressure cause.

## First-season lasting cost

During the high-stakes regional sequence Kiva will permanently lose fine sensation
in the first two fingers of her left hand. Healing closes the injury but does not
restore exact touch. The cost is planted before it occurs and changes how she grips,
trains, and asks for help. Rank cannot erase it.

## PROJECT EVIDENCE — books/book-2680-opening/packets/MOVEMENT-002.md

# Movement 002 — The Crooked Lane

- PWA book id: `kindling-2680-book-01`
- Series id: `kindling-2680`
- Author: Claude Opus 5.5
- Seat: Monroe Jackson 1.3.0 / O'Connor 1.3.0
- Chapters: 9–16, one continuous creative run
- Owner names: Kiva Rowan, Mara Rowan, Orla Dane, Anwen Pryce, Perrin Cade,
  Kellan Renn, Noa Bexley, Ysra Crest, Rhea Sorn, Hollis Vane. The owner previously
  delegated necessary continuing names; do not rename locked people or import names
  from the research corpus.

## Where we enter

Monday the fifteenth ends Movement One. Kiva has the lowest defensible place:
provisional Third Division, Crooked Line third team, Orla as accountable sponsor,
and no live-cell work until Orla certifies her in writing. Kiva's left arm is in a
sling, her knee is strained, and the first two fingers of her left hand remain dull.
She has never completed a correct cross-door handoff. The Satori disk is cold.

Anwen's Graywater field placement decision lands on the twentieth. Perrin has a
Second Division Stonehand bursary and honest envy. Kellan is First Division and has
begun comparing Kiva's data rather than dismissing it. Mara has triggered a Works
review of her tuning license. Rhea privately recognizes Corran's disk; Kiva does not
know that. The Sag Line baseline, T-0 records, Annick account, and Unroofed Sky remain
unexplained.

Read the full preceding movement and its continuity checkpoint. Carry injuries,
dates, knowledge boundaries, meters, door limits, household conditions, and every
institutional consequence forward.

## What this movement is for

Make the series' central pleasure unmistakable: serious students at discipline and
military-response schools get stronger by training, fighting one another, learning to
kill Riftspawn, and earning the finite ash that funds the next threshold.

Give several connected chapters—not a montage—to spawn-kill preparation and the
team's first licensed progression step. Let the reader learn with the students:
spawn anatomy and behavior; terrain and approach; declared kill, containment, rescue,
and recovery roles; abort calls; how dissolution becomes dangerous loose ash; how to
catch, quarantine, grade, and reconcile a yield; and why a kill can still be a failed
response. Let fear, humor, rivalry, equipment mistakes, and bodily recovery live in
the scenes. Kiva cannot enter live work until Orla has evidence sufficient to sign.

The movement should include a developed supervised Handful-yield kill and clean
recovery. The team receives its first small recorded ash share after civic and casualty
shares. Ash is measured by yield, grade, and resonance. The team must choose whether
to spend, save, or return part of its share for training cells, permitted combat ash,
equipment, travel, or medical reserve. Killing does not automatically raise rank;
the usable gain is the correction they can reproduce and the ash that funds the next
attempt.

Then deliver the first sanctioned collegiate meet. Human opponents remember, feint,
adapt, and exploit habits the spawn drills created. Give the school-versus-school
combat developed tactical length. A useful team loss should prove that access is not
control while still producing a visible, earned improvement. Noa Bexley begins
recording Kiva's output, Draw, recovery efficiency, and door behavior because the
numbers do not fit the one-door model.

Seed the coupled escalation without explaining it: this student cohort is stronger
than recent records predict, and the contained/live spawn season shows a small but
specific variation instructors do not expect. Characters may call these separate
oddities. They do not yet prove that rising Glory pressure beyond the filter causes
both.

## What must remain true / withheld

- One source, three traditions. Soul supplies owned intention and pattern; ash is
  finite ownerless fuel. Ash can power a permitted technique or device, but cannot
  buy judgment, transfer a spawn's ability, or act as automatic experience.
- Rank, Hold, Draw, control, Call, yield, grade, and resonance are distinct. Preserve
  their different meanings on the page.
- Kiva may improve single-door control and the transition drill. She does **not**
  complete the first deliberate two-door sequence in this movement. The disk stays
  cold. That payoff belongs to Movement Three.
- The permanent loss of fine sensation in two fingers remains reserved for Movement
  Five. Current dullness may complicate grip and recovery but cannot quietly become
  permanent or disappear.
- Orla's sponsorship, Mara's license review, Anwen's independent field goal, Perrin's
  bursary and envy, and Kellan's legitimate excellence all carry real consequences.
- Do not identify the cause of the Fall, prove the filter theory, explain the disk,
  name the Makers, or turn the Unroofed Sky into tactical guidance.
- Professional and collegiate standings value rescue, closure, infrastructure,
  clean ash, and violations as well as kills. Do not make kill count meaningless;
  do not make it the only score.
- Preserve clear spatial action, developed fights, narration-ready punctuation, and
  the O'Connor numerical craft targets. Do not fall into uniform one-line sentences.

## What the author owns

Own the eight-chapter route, scene boundaries, training failures, spawn expression,
school opponent, tactics, conversations, humor, ash-share decision, and the exact
shape of the useful loss. Invent new minor names only when the scene needs them and
collision-check them against the registry. Keep Kiva near the established 87% focus;
use brief secondary cutaways only where they create dramatic irony or independent
stakes.

The training and fights should feel like lived collegiate progression, not a manual.
Let readers understand the rules early enough to anticipate a tactic, then allow both
the spawn and the human opponents to make the students revise it.

## Where we leave pressure

By Chapter Sixteen, Kiva retains her roster place after a useful sanctioned loss;
Team Seven has killed and cleanly recovered its first live Handful, owns a small ash
share, and has made a consequential choice about it. Anwen's field-placement path has
moved independently. Noa holds measurements that the institution cannot dismiss.
The reader has seen why school combat and Riftspawn combat teach different things,
and has one concrete sign that both the student ceiling and the spawn season are
rising. Movement Three opens with the circuit accelerating toward a live Flask-level
response and Kiva's first true two-door sequence.

## 2. Character state at exit

**Kiva Rowan** (17). Provisional roster place, **Third Division, Crooked Line School, third team**; sponsor **O. Dane**. Tuition only, no stipend.
- Ysra's conditions: no live-cell events until sponsor certifies in writing; declare one door to the referee before every sanctioned event and declare any change; book to sponsor weekly; review after first sanctioned meet; one sanctioned-event incident ends her season. Ysra's Classification Day condition (more than one door + anything going wrong = file closed for the season) was **set aside in writing on the strength of the field book**, with the run still ruled a failure.
- Mara's conditions: never touch any cell in the flat until Mara says; report the dream the same night; book on the kitchen table nightly and Mara reads it.
- Readings (Classification Day): Draw 3.1 (worse, up from 2.9 last year); Hold Edge 3.0, Ward 2.6, Stance 6.2 (5.8 last year); Rank unsettled (frame flickers Ember/Flame/dashes); First door none. Call column dark.
- Edge: square-ended, single-edged **bar**, forearm length; edge-light dull orange, gutters; cannot reshape. Discovered use: broad flat stops narrow blades dead. Ember slab: dull copper, leather cuff, **left** forearm; bar forms on the **right** fist. Stride: 3–4 bounds. Ward: tray-sized, splashes when dropped; learning to curve/fold. Stance: her steadiest door; learned "give the ground its weight back."
- Meter: bottom heat-line (nearest breastbone) warms first; she calls "one."
- Drill (open-close-open, not a true sequence): best 9.5 s. **No correct cross-door hand-off has ever succeeded.** In the Cellhouse she reached for Stride→stance and failed the transition: she opened the still door with the Ember still caught in the doorway. Nothing passed between the doors. It held under impact once, at a cost, with the hand door deliberately shut. The page (Kiva, Orla, Rhea's card) calls it a jam, not a hand-off, and unrepeatable. **The first true two-door sequence remains a Movement Three event.**
- **Injuries:** left knee strained Wed 10th, re-strained Fri 12th (ice; don't land on it). Left forearm scraped raw from the elbow down to the edge of the cuff; cuff leather scored. **Heat-lines now run from mid-breastbone over the collarbone, past the shoulder, halfway down the outer upper arm; raised, red, two blistered patches.** Arm in sling ~1 week. **Left first and second fingers:** numb after backwash; **temporary.** Prickling by 02:44 Sat; "thin gloves" Sat morning; Monday "better than on Saturday and still not right … more each day." Medic: most of the rest over a week or two; the warning is that next time they might not come back. Recovery continues early in Movement Two. **Permanent fine-sensation loss remains the Movement Five cost.** Nosebleed during fever. Healer Aske at the Wren House is the named follow-up.
- Knows: the Annick account? **No.** The name "Unroofed Sky"? **No.** Has copied the three T-0 register lines into her book; *Why: unknown.* She does **not** conclude that anyone dreamed in 2446 or 2604: the old books show only lamps, a time and "no fault found." Has now told Mara the dream in full for the first time. Told Ysra only that the dream came, not its content.
- Disk: **cold throughout**, never warm. Swung out of her collar at about 3:34 in the Cellhouse and is visible on the run plate from then on.

**Mara Rowan** (Kindled, hand door, Flame; Grade Two Tuning License). Burn scar across right palm and inside wrist (2677 Linden Row cell). **License: one further household filing triggers automatic review — the 13th's core trip is that filing; Mara filed it herself on the morning of the 13th; Works review notice expected by end of week.** The license letter arrived in the winter; Kiva learned that only by asking on the 13th. Signed trial consent (Wed 10th) and roster consent (Sat 13th), saying aloud that roster supervision may help her license review. Apologized for withholding the letter. Has read the field book. Clerk at Hanley Street is hand-copying the fragile 2446 register entry for her.

**Orla Dane** (50s; Ember door, Edge, Torch — as far as her own door has taken her, not a cap on single-door rank; ex-Home Line field officer ~20 years; bad left knee, stick). Now Kiva's **accountable sponsor**: if Kiva has a sanctioned-event incident, Orla loses sponsoring standing and her teaching contract is reviewed. Sponsorship procedure: one two-page form and the Placement Chair's stamp (no hearing). Private history, **told in full for the first time in chapter 8** (chapters 2–3 carry only the fear and the unsigned second page): sponsored **Jessamy Hart** (twin-kindled Ember + hand, Brickfields) in 2659; put him in an ashcraft trial at the Regional in his second year; his doors fought on a live cell before ~4,000 people; he lost a hand; two students burned; file sealed; he left Meridian at nineteen; reportedly fixing pumps on the south coast; Orla never wrote. Standing suspended ten years, restored eleven years ago, never used until now. Knew Ilse Corran; saw the disk on her at the Ferren levee ~2655; has wondered about Ilse's choice of Kiva for four years (since the seal was broken). Has read the Annick notebook ("the sky unroofed") in the Compact Library; has told no one and has no proof it relates to Kiva.

**Anwen Pryce** (17; hand door, Ward + Sustain read; Flame; Hold 7.4, Draw 0.95; Wren House field track). Friends with Kiva since nine; her family runs the laundry **next door** to Rowan Instruments (the shop sits between the laundry and a bakery; all three buildings share one core). In the trial she chose the casualty over leaving and said so ("the score first"). On Graywater field officer **Doran Mace**'s list, "higher than I was." Graywater posts four field placements on the 20th. Knows Rhea Sorn asked for the run. Training at Wren House lanes; skipping Orla's Monday night; wants Thursday's drill "in nine." Private note: "I want it anyway. On my own." Voice: she owns "I want it said."

**Perrin Cade** (17; still door, Stonehand Hall; Flame, upper band; Hold 6.8). Moved by board ruling to Team Seven (resented; said so). Held ~400 kg shutter on his hands for **about forty seconds** after winch loss (3:38 to about 4:20), lying on his back along the pump-room threshold, then released by "giving it back"; individual note "exceptional." Hands purple, bandaged. **Stonehand bursary granted: Second Division, tuition and stipend.** Father (tram-yard brake mechanic) watched from the gallery. Grateful and still openly jealous. Asked to be told before being rescued.

**Kellan Renn** (17–18; Ember door, Fire; Hold 9.1, Draw 0.82; blue-grey slab on steel cuff, left forearm; long narrow single-edged blade, blue-white edge-light). **First Division, Crooked Line first team.** Mother: ash grader at the Halden refinery for 22 years; a falsified stamp nearly blamed on her when he was nine; her written number saved her. Believes measurement protects people not in charge. Not touched in a sanctioned bout since autumn until Kiva's ½ off the riser. Paper note on Kiva in his pocket ("Dram 0.88 to my 0.31"). In the trial: held the stair from release 2, declared and took the quench by vaulting from the rail to the mezzanine edge, dropped back to the floor and broke the husk on Kiva with a dark blade; scraped right knee. Told the booth he trusted Kiva's numbers. "I'll be comparing."

**Ysra Crest** (~60; hand door, Torch; ex-Wren House ward-holder; Placement Chair, 19 years running the Cellhouse, 11 master-stop pulls). Kept her hand on the master stop from 8.0 and did not pull it. Ruled provisional Third Division on the strength of the book, while stating the run failed.

**Rhea Sorn** (41; Graywater; Ember door, Edge only, Torch — as far as her door has taken her; 23 years; draft board six seasons; white streak at left temple; pale grey eyes). Reviewed Team Seven from the three-and-a-half-minute mark on the silent run plate Saturday evening, with the booth's notes for the calls. Draft card (in tray): *Rowan, K. Unclassified. Placement not yet posted. NOT for draft this season. Watch first sanctioned meet. Stood a husk off a teammate on two doors jammed together. Not a hand-off, and not to be expected twice. Kept the third shut under impact. Called a quench from under it and was right. Responder.* Private card (in her pocket, not filed): *Corran's disk.* At nineteen, seconded to a Home Line crew in the Ferren culvert floods, she was pulled out of a culvert by Ilse Corran and saw the disk then. Has not contacted Kiva. She does not yet know the placement ruling.

**Ilse Corran** (prior bearer; d. 2673). Home Line field officer; tall, cropped grey hair, long jaw scar; friend of Ione Rowan; carried the disk ~30 years; appointed Kiva after Ione's death (2669), sealed until the Kindling day; delivered by a Home Line courier at 07:00 on the 2676 Kindling morning.

**Ione Rowan** (Kiva's mother, Mara's sister; d. 2669). Eastbank substation tech; Tallow substation runaway on cut ash stamped First warped the gate; she quenched by hand; the fire killed her.

Minor named: Brannock (rushing municipal operator, taught by Orla), Tobin (van driver), Mrs. Pryce (burn salve), Healer Aske (Wren House), Doran Mace (Graywater field officer).

## 3. World facts established on the page

- **Circuit axes:** Rank, Hold (out of ten), Draw (ash per standard work vs 1.0; lower is better), Call (team judgment; dark on Classification Day). Projection boards; draw bench (lamp-cell, sticky needle at "two"); hold posts; lit frame. No overlay.
- **Divisions:** First, Second, Third. Third = tuition only. Provisional placements carry a sponsor line.
- **Doors in 2680 speech:** Ember door / hand door / still door. Sustain is glossed on the page as the healing side of the hand door (the read of where a body is hurt). Kindling stations: Ember slab in a brazier, bowl of rift-pool water (Wren House), stance stone ("five hundred years of feet"); then the lit frame.
- **Ash terms (updated canon):** **yield** is quantity (Handful, Flask, Barrel, Well); **grade** is purity/stability (First, Second, Third); resonance is not yet on the page. The manuscript uses "a Handful" and "a Barrel yield / Barrel-yield" for spawn size and keeps "Grade First/Second/Third" for cells and stamps.
- **Open lane:** Stonehand's Terrace Ring — sunken bowl, three knee-high steps a pace and a half deep, ~12-pace raked sand floor, rim sensing mast, standard dram of clean ash settled before bouts; dark Edge only; 1 point body/head, ½ limb; three wins. Board marks a requested bout with the word *requested*.
- **Cellhouse:** ex-refinery, ~40 × 14 paces; Gate and green line west; gallery behind glass along the north wall (about four hundred public seats, assessors' booth with master stop, recruiters' section); mezzanine substation east, **2.5 m** off the floor (12-step stair on the **south** wall rising east, wall on the left and rail on the right for someone facing down it; drum regulator, red quench with wire seal, quench box of wet salt, load board on the east wall, winch on the same circuit); pump room under the mezzanine with a jammed shutter (~400 kg) and one wide west-facing doorway — from the mezzanine rail Kiva can see the threshold and Perrin, not the inside; chute mid-south wall from **the Kennel**, a bottle-rift in the basement vault in a Wren House–maintained lattice impressed by a Glory master ~60 years ago. Trial husks are dog-sized, a **Handful** each in the yield books; **four releases to a run**. Objectives 1–4 and violations as briefed by Ysra. Breaker trip at 8.5 = uncontrolled; breaker cuts load but **does not stop a pattern; only the quench does.** After the run, clerks gather the husk ash into sealed tins for the Wren House graders.
- **Cells:** a cell keeps what you leave in it, including mood (Brannock's "hurry"). Kiva's three-door contamination persisted after release and **called husks** (rift attraction) — never before seen in the Compact; nobody predicted it.
- **Paper law:** every serious ash device has a chained paper cell book, "since the first licensed cells, more than four hundred years ago"; the *Transient Register* records unexplained flickers (more than four hundred years of volumes). **T-0** = transient, no cause. Three multi-grid T-0s: **2446** (night, every lamp, every district; fragile volume, copy pending), **2604** (03:10, Compact Quarter; margin: "Again. Same as the '46 entry in the old book. Every lamp at once."), **2676** (14:52, Eastbank + Lantern + Weighbridge grids; Kiva's Kindling). The 13th's trip was **local only** (one shared core).
- **Sag Line masts:** baselines creeping upward on every mast for years; recalibration masks it; Mara declined to recalibrate mast six. Measurement only — no theory confirmed. "The filter" is glossed once as the old idea that the sky has a lid that is wearing thin.
- **Institutions/colours:** Graywater (slate blue, white wave; hall on the Brickfields road), Saltmere (green), Halden companies (black and gold); Order of the Even Hand (Ward devotion, one door for life; Annick reached Glory). City banner: *SIX HUNDRED YEARS. WE WROTE IT DOWN.*
- **The crooked post:** last post in the Old Yard lane, black, split, two iron bands, leans left. Standard manual: "retained for ceremonial continuity" (since the 2603 committee vote, 11–6). Orla: "There used to be one. It's not in the manual anymore." Marisol's pencil note survives in the superseded stacks.

## 4. Prologue canon touched (for owner review)

- No present-trilogy figure is dramatized. Snapshot 4 shows initials only (T.V., J., D.W., K., S.V.) inside a copied ledger. **The T.V. / T. Vey echo is left as ambiguity and is not confirmed** (owner ruling).
- Failing-filter idea appears first as post-Homura hearsay in 2152 ("the sky had a lid… the bombs cracked the lid"), never confirmed.
- Snapshot 4 stays dated **2152** (owner ruling).
- Corbel Reach access history, now consistent: the 2081 meltdown (diesel ran out in the second winter after the Fall); before 2170 only scavengers went closer than the far bank and came home sick (the 2150 crew worked the far-bank towns); 2170 is the first sanctioned entry (four people, Home Line rotation); 2195 Unit One with a 48-cartridge ash lattice set by six Kindled and checked by a Blaze ward master (no Glory figure onstage); cartridge 31 failure → "Check the pairs" opens every later shutdown code; ran 19 years.
- Abel's rotation numbers: Ines out at 41, recalled to 9; "you come out at forty-one again."
- Tamar Vey: large, slow, limping former Home Line ward-holder (hand door), letters-and-reasons teacher; the weir story and the bowl/copper/stone/ash demonstration (fence holds ~50 breaths after release, until the pinch is spent). He leaves; Gideon never sees him again. Gideon's question entry is the only record.
- 2247: Halden-licensed Class C domestic core; master pattern impressed by an unseen Glory master.
- 2362: the Ferren spawn is a **Barrel yield**; the lost recovery is "one full Barrel" of clean ash (the first draft's "200 bbl" is gone).
- **Hesper Annick:** Even Hand master, Glory 2431, the flood-wall ward, walked out in the winter rifts of 2461, recorded dead by the Order; notebook c. 2446–2450 with the fever account and the phrase "the sky unroofed." **The register's 2446 T-0 was placed deliberately to sit inside that span.** Neither Kiva nor Mara knows Annick exists. Only the reader can make the link; the actual pre-Kiva dream fragment is reserved for Movement Four (owner ruling).
- The Kindling (2676): disk delivered that morning; Ember opens at the brazier (slab turns dull copper), hand door at the bowl (water rises), still door at the stone; frame cannot settle; three heat-lines appear; the dream comes while the disk goes "colder"; yard lamps blink; "Classification deferred … There isn't a column for this."
- Left as drafted, for the owner: in snapshot 1 the white light and the first colours are seen a few lines before the city goes dark.

## 5. Plants and open threads carried forward

| Plant | Status at exit |
|---|---|
| Disk warms only after first correct deliberate sequence | Never warm. Orla's definition is on the page: a sequence passes one door's work into the next without dropping it; the drill is not a sequence; the Cellhouse landing was a failed transition. |
| First true two-door sequence | Not achieved. Reserved for Movement Three. |
| Crooked post's reason | Retired 2603; Orla knows it once existed; Marisol's pencil note survives. |
| Sag Line baseline creep | Recorded, unexplained. |
| T-0 entries (2446/2604/2676) | Copied by Kiva; 2446 copy pending from the clerk. Evidence only; no explanation drawn. |
| Annick notebook | Seen by the reader (2603) and Orla; Kiva has not seen it. Movement 4 fragment is still available. |
| Rhea's "Corran's disk" card | Private; unrevealed. |
| Mara's license review | Notice due end of week; outcome open. |
| Anwen's Graywater placement | Decided on the 20th. |
| Kellan's comparison | First Division vs Kiva's Third. |
| Two-finger sensation | Temporary dullness, recovering; a warning. Permanent loss remains reserved for Movement 5. |
| Jessamy Hart | Alive, south coast, uncontacted. |
| Cell contamination as a three-door failure mode | Demonstrated once; Compact has no procedure for it. |
| Clean ash from trial kills | Gathered and tinned by clerks for grading; Team Seven has no yield share yet (Movement Two). |

## 6. Deliberately not done

The disk never warms, speaks, or teaches. No correct cross-door hand-off succeeds. No rank is revealed. The Unroofed Sky is not named to Kiva and supplies no tactic. The filter, the Makers, the disk's origin, and the cause of the Fall stay unresolved. Noa Bexley and Hollis Vane do not appear. Movement Two has not been started.

## 7. New names introduced (collision-checked against the locked list)

Prologue: Ruth Achterberg, Mina, Joel, Saffi, Ben, Piotr Dabrowski; Teo Barrasa, Marguerite, Lina, Farrow; Pell Garrow, Nico, Henrik Garrow, Aud Fenn, Sefton; Oriel Hask, Brother Aldous; Ines Coyle, Abel Strand, Linnet, Dov; Tamar Vey (locked), Gideon Sayle; Pia Morrow, Rao; **Femi Oduya** (replaces Bram Oduya; "Bram" collides with Kindled canon — "Femi" has no match anywhere in this repository or in the research corpus as of 2026-10-10), Hester Quayle, Jory Quayle, Mr. Pask; Tova Reinholt, Hob Lanyard, Ansel, Mags, Kaspar; Marisol Quenby, Teague Orme, Warden Ottilie Sand, Hesper Annick.
Movement: Ione Rowan, Ilse Corran, Jessamy Hart, Brannock, Tobin, Doran Mace, Healer Aske.
Places/things: Garrow's Ford, Lowmarket, Corbel Reach, the Tarrow, Pellow Building, Weighbridge Street, the Lantern, Ferren valley, Saltmere, Vellen company, Coper Street, Tallis Street substation, Tallow substation, Linden Row, Hanley Street, Brickfields road, the Kennel, the Terrace Ring.

# Kindling 2680 — Book One, Movement Two — continuity checkpoint

Edition: first draft, 2026-10-10, chapters 9–16. Written by the drafting author
(Claude Opus 5.5, `claude-opus-5-5`) directly after the run, from the manuscript as
drafted. **Not reviewed, repaired, or scored.** Where this file and the manuscript ever
disagree, the manuscript governs. This file is not manuscript. It carries forward
`provenance/MOVEMENT-001-CONTINUITY.md`; anything not restated here stands as recorded
there.

### The meet (Sat 4th) — one pass, all numbers agree

Rules as stated by the referee: three carries of four minutes; litter wholly on your own
porch, 3 points and the carry ends; a clean body or head contact puts a fighter **down**,
1 point, ten-count kneeling; a limb contact is a half, two halves on one fighter in one
carry make a down; still-doors score with the open hand or a throw that puts both
shoulders on the sand; Embers dark; at time, litter on the sand scores 1 to the half it
lies in; one standard dram each for the whole lane, overspend is a violation and a point
off. Kiva may not open a newly declared door **until the referee's white flag is up**.

| Carry | Kiva declares | Crooked Line | Stonehand | Kiva's dram (cumulative) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Still; one change (to Ember) | 2: Jude downs the first-year; Jude downs Moss. (Noa a half on the first-year.) | 4: Keld downs Kiva in her change gap; litter carried (about 2:50). | 0.38 |
| 2 | Ember; two changes (to still, back to Ember) | 2: Moss, offered; Keld, late. (Jude a half on Brask's forearm.) | 4: Brask puts Kiva on her back when she goes dark; litter carried. (Keld a half on Kiva's arm.) | 0.87 |
| 3 | Still; **no change** | 3: Jude downs Brask in Kiva's stop; Noa's two halves down the first-year; litter in the western half at time. | 1: Keld downs Pru (ward held nine taps). | 0.98, no overspend |
| **Final** | three declared changes, none before the flag, none undeclared, **no incident** | **7** | **9** | |

Hold-post readings directly after, by the referee's leave (Classification or spring figure
in brackets): Kiva Edge-light 13 counts (11); ward 7 taps (6); stance 4.9 (6.2). Jude Edge
hold 4.1 (6.6). Pru ward 3.8 (5.9). Noa 2.7 (4.0). Kiva's still-door draw across carry
three: **0.93**.

## 2. Character state at exit

**Kiva Rowan.** Provisional Third Division, Crooked Line third team, **retained** at
Ysra's review on the 6th; all of Ysra's conditions stand unchanged. Orla's certification
(the 25th): *live work in the recovery role only: dark hands, under a declared team call,
no door opened within three paces of open ash or of any charged cell. Not certified: Kill,
Hold, Carry, or tuning of any kind. To be reviewed after her first field response.* Orla
has not added a line since ("you'll kneel for" the Flask).
- Skills gained: the catch (seals by the lid's click and by the third finger of the left
  hand; calls *seal check*); setting the mark by reading ground and wind; calling *dark*;
  holding all three doors shut under fear by **calling a number** (her own discovery: the
  doors come through when she stops counting, whether she is frightened or pleased; in
  the Cellhouse she stopped calling at 7.3). Single door held through a four-minute carry
  with no change. Heel braced on a riser, now used with the still door.
- Drill: 9.5 → 8.8 (24th) → 8.6 (3rd) → **8.4 s** (6th). No-shape drill 7.0 s (18th). She
  no longer watches the metal home. It is still a drill, not a sequence.
- **No deliberate two-door sequence was attempted or completed.** In the meet's third
  carry her stop and Jude's touch made a hand-off across two people; Orla told her
  explicitly it is not hers and not a sequence. She now knows what it feels like from the
  inside.
- Sense without a door: she feels open ash "listening," felt Kellan's print as a "draft,"
  and feels skin ash as "curved." Graders cannot feel this through a tin.
- Numbers: bench Draw still 3.1 on record. Low Lane mast, still door, four minutes under
  load: 0.09 dram. Stonehand board, still door, carry three: Draw 0.93.
- **Injuries:** sling off Fri 19th. Heat-lines healed to three raised threads; **they kept
  their new length** (to mid upper arm) and did not draw back; stretch twice a day. Left
  knee: still no hard landings; sore after license day; strapped after the meet. Left
  first and second fingers: **still dull and still recovering.** Aske on the 29th: the
  first nearly true, the second about a week behind, both "reporting late"; another month
  before she has to think to notice; "it won't do it twice," and next time the same hour,
  with ward-trace ash for the easing. On the 4th her left hand gave out without warning
  twelve seconds from time. **Permanent loss remains the Movement Five cost.**
- The dream has not recurred. Nothing further learned about the T-0 entries, Annick, or
  the disk. She has noticed that she has stopped asking the disk for a verdict.
- Disk: **cold throughout.** Checked on the Kennel stair (25th), on the Sag Line (28th),
  and in the Old Yard (6th). Kennel ash from the trial is still in three grooves.
- Does not know why Rhea Sorn was in the Stonehand gallery.

**Mara Rowan.** Works notice (19th): shall not tune, refill or seal any licensed cell;
instrument repair and ledger reconciliation continue at the Works' discretion; all shop
books to Examiner Rusk on the 9th; must state whether the 2677 household condition
persists and furnish the sponsor's certification status. Tuning was "a third of the
money." She has copied Orla's certificate for Rusk. The dead flat panel is tagged
*QUENCHED 13TH. NOT REFILLED PENDING REVIEW.* New on the page: the middle of her scarred
right palm has had no feeling since 2677; she trained her left hand to read. She read
mast six's drum for the Home Line on the 28th, told Vane her license was under review,
and watched the response from the berm; she came to the meet and sat beside Orla. Vane's
half-sheet to the Works is under the brass weight with the notice. Outcome open.

**Orla Dane.** Used the sponsor's-floor rule (a sponsor may walk an uncertified candidate
onto a contained floor once, under the sponsor's own Edge; any incident is the sponsor's).
Her edge-light is a deep, steady lamp-oil yellow. Her knee held for the kill and two steps
and folded on the third; bound in flannel through the 6th. She has taught for twenty-one
years. She declined to hurry for Mara's hearing, Anwen's bell, or Kellan's place, and
declined to warn Kiva about human opponents before the meet. Took Hollis Vane down his
first culvert when he was nineteen, and fell in it.

**Anwen Pryce.** **Graywater field placement, ward line, fourth of four**, condition met
on the 28th. Chose Team Seven over Team Eleven at the bell, having said in advance that she
would go if Seven was not whole. Called the team's aborts (contained floor, the 27th; field,
the 28th). Changed how she holds a ward after watching the Home Line hold ("under it, not
behind it"). Vane told her he would tell Mace what he saw. First shadow rotation: night
watch on the Ferren culverts from the evening of the 5th. Her "nine" is said and written.

**Perrin Cade.** Stonehand Second Division. Palms unwrapped the 20th, yellow-green; **split
again on the 28th**, with two ribs cracked (not broken through), when he stood dark in a
husk's line to save the cloth, after telling Kiva first. Packed the faulty Stonehand kit
for the 27th and owns it; bought the team's own kit. **Was to have tried for the Hall's
first-year load plate on the 4th and was withdrawn for his ribs**; three others took the
mark. He has written it in his own book and told Kiva not to put it in hers. Did not shout
a warning from the gallery; Lowen Brask thanked him for that. Lowen Brask was his first
teacher at the Hall.

**Kellan Renn.** First Division. **Declined the board-approved transfer to Team Nineteen**
(reasons given: Nineteen does not catch; it would be a ruling and not a number; he wants a
second go with the same people). Printed the team's first contained Handful by standing lit
within three paces through the count, and had it entered as his. Now takes his mark from
the catcher, stands dark at six paces, and counts aloud with Kiva. Lit after his foot on
the field kill. Nearly lit on the second husk and stopped when Kiva said "count." Vane:
"the best blade that has come through this gap today." His mother kept a reference jar for
every batch she flagged; the jar was re-measured eleven months later. Won his First
Division lane on the 4th (four downs, untouched). Is tracking Kiva's low spend: her carry
three was 0.11 in four minutes against 0.19 for his best carry.

**Noa Bexley** (first appearance; written as he/him by author's choice; Crooked Line third
team; Ember door, Stride mostly, weak Edge; keeps the team's tapes). Has a notebook page
headed *Things that are probably nothing* (two tears in nine days; skinned Handfuls; the
class medians; the Low Lane reading) and draws no line between its entries. Asked Kiva's
consent before filing anything and asked for his name second. Kiva's terms: every figure
in her book the same day, she and Orla see the sheet first, and he stops filing if she
says so. Ysra's order: continue under her office's notice, on sanctioned instruments, with
Kiva's written consent each month, copy to the Chair.

**Ysra Crest.** Retained Kiva's place; conditions unchanged. Could not dismiss Noa's sheet
because it comes off a board her own office certified, and declined to rule on what it
means. Has two reports on her desk from two offices (a strong class; a dirty spring) and
says only that neither has asked to see the other.

**Rhea Sorn.** Watched the meet from the Stonehand gallery. Did not approach Kiva. Saw the
cord at Kiva's collar and nothing on it. Second draft card: *lost 9–7; three declared
changes, each cost her; third carry one door, no change; asked to her face to open a
second and did not; still NOT for draft; watch the Flask*; and a separate line on the
spring (nine tears on the district board since the thaw against a usual four or five;
three of Graywater's threw skinned Handfuls). The private "Corran's disk" card is still
private. She has decided to wait at least until the Flask.

**Hollis Vane** (first appearance; written as he/him by author's choice; about 45; Home
Line watch officer for the Sag Line this month; slate on a belt cord; his own door is not
shown). Downs one member of every team mid-response. The Home Line keeps four columns:
was it closed, who was hurt, what still works in the morning, what was tinned. Initialed
Kiva's "curved" report on the back of the top tin tag as the second such report this
month. Wrote to the Works about mast six. Did not see or mention the disk.

## 3. New people and places

Crooked Line third team: **Jude Talley** (captain; third-year, 19 or 20; Ember, Edge hold
about 6.5, Flame since sixteen; chipped front tooth), **Pru Finch** (hand door, Ward;
narrates disasters), Noa Bexley. Their litter dummy is "Donny."
Kennel: **Warden Oswin Druce** (Wren House; about 55; grey-stained fingertips; has kept
the Kennel book 22 of its 60 years).
Team Nineteen: **Selka Dray** (Second Division, Saltmere band, cropped red hair, yellow
edge-light; the Ember Perrin beat 3–2 in the open lane).
Home Line: **Bettan** (grey-haired catcher who weighs on the bank). Walk-ons heard on
license day: Lund (Team 29's ward), Miss Corwen (a Stonehand Ember).
Sag Line: **Agnes Roake** (plot nineteen; allotment pump warden; 400 seedlings in trays).
Wren House: **Grader Halloran** (19 years a grader); **Healer Aske** (appears; no pronoun
used).
Stonehand third team: **Lowen Brask** (captain; third-year; Hold 7.5; taught the junior
lanes), **Idony Keld** (long-armed; feints at the knee), **Hendry Moss**, and an unnamed
first-year with jug-handle ears. Their dummy is "Old Tom." Perrin's borrowed sandbag is
"Gerald."
Works: **Examiner Rusk** (by letter only).
Places: the **Low Lane** (third team's room, an old cart shed beside the school's core
house; brick floor falling a hand's width to a drain; sixty-year-old wooden-cased mast
with one pen); the Kennel **cast room** and **settling floor** (24 paces long; three
drained tanks each a pace deep, six by three, with a grate at the low end; plank bridges;
north gutter with four open grates; double steel door and lever; yellow line at the west
end) and **cold room**; the Wren House **grading room** (no lamps or cells within thirty
paces; seven cups; the reference shelf); the Compact **ash office** in the Measure Hall
basement; the **roster corridor** drawers of Classification Day summary sheets; the Sag
Line **allotments** (drainage cut two paces wide at the foot of the berm; plank bridge;
plot nineteen with tarred shed, potato bed, cinder path firm as far as the shed; pump
house forty paces east of the hold); Stonehand's **Long Terrace** (sunk a story; 40 by 14
paces; a two-step porch at each end; a low step along both long walls; a stone sill
across the middle) and its first-year **load plate**.

All new names were checked against `universe/NAME_REGISTRY.md`, the other books'
registries, this book's files, and the locked cast list before use; none matched.

## 4. World facts established on the page

- **Two team structures.** A student's roster place (school and division) is for meets and
  Measures. The **field license** is taken in the mixed trial teams, which keep their
  numbers and hold the yield book. First step ("the Handful license"): contained floors at
  the Kennel until the warden passes a team as fit, then one field Handful at a **held
  tear** under a chartered officer, called in trial-score order. The Home Line holds a
  small tear open inside a four-person ward square for a day or so, and each team takes
  one husk as the tear gives it (about every forty minutes on the 28th). Three held tears
  licensed the cohort this spring: Ferren culverts, 14th–15th (teams 1–12); river flats,
  22nd (13–26); Sag Line, 28th (call positions 27–40, which by then included Team Nineteen's repeat at
  39th, plus Team Eleven's reseat). First Division fighters need the first step to enter live lanes.
- **The husk.** Nothing inside; a pattern pressed into loose ash with nobody home, tied at
  **the knot** (heart height on a dog). A lit Edge through the knot is the clean kill; a
  hard dark blow can shake the shape out of a Handful. Limbs are "lent." It reads rather
  than sees, turns to the brightest worked power within about twelve paces, goes straight,
  does not feint, and does not remember. With nothing lit, it stops and then seeks the
  nearest pattern (the Kennel lattice; a hold wall).
- **The four roles:** Kill, Hold, Carry, Catch, declared aloud. A team that does three has
  failed its response. **Abort:** any member, any time, *off* three times; everyone goes
  dark and walks to the line; called early it costs nothing.
- **The count.** From the cut, fresh ash is **open** for about sixty counts and will take
  the first pattern that touches it. Inside the count and inside three paces nothing is
  lit: **dark hands**. Inside the count the catcher owns the floor and may call *dark*.
  A lit door near open ash **prints** it (a hair-bright line in the color of that person's
  light; the tin hums and is warm after a night); printed ash is salted. **Loose ash finds
  the nearest lamp:** open ash leans and drifts toward the nearest worked pattern, and in
  wind it travels; it fouled the allotment pump cell on the 28th. Ash tinned inside the
  count settles clean; ash still lying open at sixty settles to what it lies on and is
  **sweepings**, Grade Third at best, not counted toward the mark. All tins spend a night
  in a cold room before grading. This also explains the Cellhouse yield line *T7, tin 4:
  salted, unread*.
- **Kit:** waxed cloth two paces square with a lead weight in each corner; two steel pans
  and a brush; squat tins of six drams with waxed-cord seals and paper tags; a box of
  **wet** quench salt.
- **Measures.** A Handful is **24 drams** by the book. **Pass:** 20 tinned at Grade Second
  or better, nothing printed, nothing fouled, nobody hurt. **Yield** by scale. **Grade** by
  how long a pinch holds a test tune: First past 100 counts, Second past 60, Third below,
  road-lamp ash under 30. **Resonance** on "the comb," nine reeds cut to families of
  pattern (edge, stride, ward, healer's read, stance, and four unnamed); Handful ash has
  always read **plain**. Resonance says which work the ash will take to; it is not a
  transferred power.
- **Shares:** civic power half; casualty reserve a quarter of the remainder; schools half
  of what is left, by head; team the rest. For 20.8: 10.4 / 2.6 / 3.9 / **3.9**. Team ash
  is held sealed at the ash office on a team book; every entry needs all four signatures.
  Posted prices: Kennel floor 0.7 a release; new catch kit 1.0; meet reserve up to 0.5 per
  fighter above the standard dram; healer's easing 1.0 a sitting; travel warrant to a
  regional qualifier 2.0 a head; return to the reference shelf or civic stock at par,
  credited. A Flask is twenty Handfuls.
- **Team Seven's entry (signed Wed 1st):** 0.5 returned to the Wren House reference shelf;
  1.0 sealed medical reserve, kept ward-trace; 1.0 catch kit; 1.4 two Kennel floors. No
  meet reserve. One of the two floors may already be owed; neither is shown used.
- **Sag Line masts:** the bell is set to a mark on the grid, so an uncorrected (elevated)
  baseline rings sooner. Masts five and seven were recalibrated in the winter. Mast six,
  left alone by Mara with her reason written, rang four minutes ahead and gave the hold
  ninety seconds in hand. This is a measurement only; no theory is drawn from it.
- **The casualty reserve** refills a fouled civic cell by claim, in about a month.
- **Sponsor's floor** rule, as above.
- **The Carry** and the Long Terrace, as in section 1.

## 5. The two oddities (seeded, unexplained, unconnected on the page)

**Spawn season.** A **skin**: a faint sheen over a Handful's front end and shoulders that
rings like a ward, turns a lit blade, and keeps itself toward the brightest lit thing
(a little slower to a ward than to a blade). Kennel book: the 4th, 12th, 17th and 27th of
the month; none in sixty years before; Druce has seen it at Flask twice in his life. One
on the Sag Line on the 28th; Bettan thinks she had one off the Ferren hold on the 14th.
Its ash reads **W-trace** and a new card on the reference shelf is dated the 5th. Team
Seven's jar is the first from the field. The board's answer so far: a drifting lattice.
Tears: three held in a fortnight; Rhea counts nine on the district board since the thaw
against a usual four or five. On the 28th the tear, regular at about forty minutes all day, gave
a husk eleven minutes after the one before. Called "a dirty spring."

**Cohort.** Class of 2680: median Hold 6.1 against forty years at 5.3–5.5; three Fire
readings at seventeen against two in the previous ten years; lowest Draw 0.7, the best
since 2651; Stonehand reports eleven candidates at or above upper Flame against a usual
three or four. Stonehand's first-year load plate (340 kilos, thirty counts, 2649) was
beaten on the 4th by 350, 355 and 362. Called "a strong year."

Noa has both on one notebook page and Ysra has both on one desk. **Nobody connects them,
and nothing on the page proves a common cause.**

| First true two-door sequence | Not attempted. Reserved for Movement Three. The across-people hand-off in carry three is the felt reference. |
| Doors rest separately when shut cleanly | One day's sanctioned readings. Noa to continue; rotation in a long event is implied, not demonstrated. |
| Bench Draw 3.1 versus single-door 0.93 | On Ysra's desk; not ruled on. |

| Two-finger sensation | Recovering, still late; failed once under load. Permanent loss reserved for Movement Five. |
| Medical reserve, 1.0 dram W-trace, sealed | Unspent. Aske has said what it is for. |

| Skin ash "curved" | In the Kennel book three times in the same words; on a tin tag in Vane's hand. |
| Mara's license | Hearing the 9th, Examiner Rusk. Vane's note filed. Open. |

| Kellan | Stayed on Seven by choice; two Kennel floors bought to practice the skin cut. |

| Hollis Vane | Knows Orla; may borrow the tin drill. No disk knowledge shown. |

| T-0 entries, Annick notebook, crooked post, Jessamy Hart, Sag Line baseline cause | Untouched in this movement. |

## 7. Deliberately not done

The disk never warms, speaks, or teaches. No deliberate two-door sequence is attempted,
and none succeeds. The finger dullness neither disappears nor becomes permanent. The
Unroofed Sky does not recur, is not named, and supplies no tactic. The cause of the Fall,
the filter theory, the disk's origin and the Makers are not explained. No kill raises
anyone's rank. The skin and the strong class are not linked by any character. Rhea does
not speak to Kiva. Hollis Vane does not recognize the disk. Movement Three has not been
started.

## 8. Items for owner or editorial attention

1. Noa Bexley's and Hollis Vane's pronouns were chosen by the author.
2. The month is given thirty days.
3. Secondary viewpoint is about 10.4% by section word count (Orla 726, Anwen 919, Kellan
   1,040, Noa 1,190, Perrin 785, Rhea 725), under the 13% target; Kiva about 89.6%.
4. The formula targets have not been measured for these chapters.
5. Chapter 9 runs to ten short sections and chapter 13 to eleven; scene length against the
   ~950-word proxy should be looked at in review.
6. Team Eleven's reseated hand-door and Team Nineteen's later history are left open.
7. The second purchased Kennel floor has no date.
